Evaluation internal:
```
def sparse_score(pos, c, n, norm=False):
    k = pos.size
    if k == 0: return inf
    sums = pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]
    prods = c[:, None] * c[None, :]
    b = np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=prods.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1).astype(float)
    s = c.sum()
    if s <=0: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*b.max()/(s*s))
```
But if we keep c normalized sum=1, score=2n*max_b.

We have positions p_i unique. Count array m[λ] = number of ordered pairs (i,j) with p_i+p_j=λ. For uniform heights c_i=1/k, q_λ=m_λ/k^2. Max q = max m / k^2. Score=2n max m / k^2.

If we move position p_a to q (not occupied), we can update counts:
- Remove all pairs involving a: for each i, old = p_a + p_i, m[old] -=1; also pairs (i,a) are included in same loop? Since we sum ordered pairs, if we loop over i all, we remove (a,i) for i all. The pairs (i,a) are separate terms but when i=a? Let's maintain ordered counts by for each i,j. Removing a: for all i:
   m[p_a + p_i] -= 1 (term (a,i))
   if i != a: m[p_i + p_a] -= 1 (term (i,a)) but p_i+p_a same sum, so total subtract 2 for i!=a, 1 for i=a.
So update: m[2*p_a] -=1; for i != a: m[p_a+p_i]-=2.
- Add new q: m[2*q]+=1; for i != a: m[q+p_i]+=2.
If we also adjust k same. Need ensure q within [0,n) and not occupied.

Sparse bincount with positions support and weights c (sum=1):
`sums = pos[:,None]+pos[None,:]`, `prods = c[:,None]*c[None,:]`, `b=bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=prods.ravel(), minlength=2N-1)`, score=2N*b.max()`.
This exact for full sequence (zeros elsewhere).

Let's design optimization to reduce score from random:

1. Start with best random candidate among several k and seeds.
2. Optimize weights (heights) for fixed support using projected gradient or mass transfer. This can reduce max without moving support.
3. Optionally optimize support positions: add/remove/move peaks (random draws) to reduce max.

Weight optimization for fixed support:
Objective f(c)=max_λ q_λ(c), with c≥0, sum=1. We can use log-sum-exp smoothing and projected gradient (simplex projection). Since support size m maybe ~1000, O(m^2) per gradient. We can do maybe thousands iterations. But random already below target; weight opt can provide margin and maybe lower score.

Implement simplex projection efficiently:
```
def project_simplex(v, s=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float).copy()
    if v.size == 0: return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    r = np.arange(1, v.size+1)
    cond = (u - (cssv - s) / r) > 0
    rho = np.argmax(cond)
    theta = (cssv[rho] - s) / (rho+1)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
Need no lambda. Good.

Objective smooth: `f_beta(c) = (1/beta) logsumexp(beta*(b - b.max()))` maybe to avoid overflow. But computing b full each iteration O(m^2). We can compute b via bincount. For gradient: `weights = softmax(beta * (b - b.max()))` length 2N-1 (~11163). Then grad_s = 2 * ∑_t c_t weights[pos_s+pos_t]. Compute with matrix: `mat = weights[pos_s[:,None]+pos_t[None,:]]` (m x m), `grad = 2 * (mat @ c)`? Let's check: grad_s = 2 ∑_t c_t w_{p_s+p_t}. Yes `grad = 2 * (W @ c)`, where W[s,t]=w[p_s+p_t]`. This is O(m^2) to build W plus matrix-vector O(m^2). Could avoid W matrix by for each s sum, but vectorized good. Memory m^2. For m=2000 4M floats 32MB, okay.

But b dependency also via c? For gradient of f = logsumexp(β b), ∂f/∂c = (1/β) * (1/Z) ∑ exp(βb_λ) ∂b_λ/∂c = ∑ w_λ ∂b_λ/∂c = grad as above without 1/β? If f= (1/β) logsumexp(β b), gradient = ∑ softmax(βb) ∂b. So no 1/β. w = softmax(βb) (subtract max). Good.

Then step: c_new = project_simplex(c - lr * grad). Since c on simplex, grad sum? Not necessarily. Choose lr. We can do backtracking line search evaluating objective (or score) exactly for a few steps. But each line search uses extra O(m^2). We can just use fixed lr and accept if exact score improved.

Mass transfer algorithm for fixed support:
Input pos, c normalized.
Loop time:
- Compute b and current score via `score_from_b(b,N)` (2N*max_b).
- lam = argmax b.
- Compute partner c for lam: For support positions, need map position to index. `pos_to_idx = np.searchsorted(pos, lam - pos)`? Since pos sorted. `target = lam - pos`. `valid = (target>=0)&(target<pos[-1]? actually pos max)`, and exact equality. Since pos sorted unique, `idx = np.searchsorted(pos, target)`, valid = idx < m and pos[idx]==target. `grad = np.zeros(m); grad[valid]=2*c[idx]` (this is ∂b_lam/∂c).
- Find `i_out = np.argmax(grad)` (largest contribution), `i_in = np.argmin(grad)` (could be 0; if all grad >0, maybe choose small). If grad[i_in] >= grad[i_out]? no improvement.
- For a sequence of fractions α of c[i_out], try transfer to i_in: c2 = c; c2[i_out]-=α*c[i_out]; c2[i_in]+=α*c[i_out]. Compute score if b update? Exact compute O(m^2). Try α in [0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]. Pick first improvement.
- If no improvement, try other i_in top few low grad or i_out top.
- Also can optimize by solving for transfer amount analytically for this lag only: q_lam quadratic in α; but other lags may increase. Use line search.

This requires O(m^2) per α. If m=1200, 1.4M; many line searches maybe okay for thousands but each 3-5ms. We have 1800s, can do 100k? Actually 1.4M bincount maybe 2ms; 1000 iterations*4=4000 evals=8s. Good. But Python overhead maybe 20ms, still okay 100k? We can limit.

2. `sparse_score(pos, c, n)` :
```
def sparse_score(pos, c, n):
    m = pos.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    s = c.sum()
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    # c normalized? return 2n*maxb/s^2
    sums = pos[:, None] + pos[None, :]
    prods = c[:, None] * c[None, :]
    b = np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=prods.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1)
    return float(2.0 * n * b.max() / (s*s))
```
Could improve by using `np.multiply.outer(c, c)`? `c[:,None]*c[None,:]`.
Need ensure pos dtype int not negative.

3. `sparse_score_from_sums(pos, sums, c, n)` to reuse sums:
```
def sparse_score_with_sums(pos, sums, c, n):
    m = pos.size
    prods = c[:,None] * c[None,:]
    b = np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=prods.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1)
    return float(2.0*n*b.max()/(c.sum()**2)), b
```
Return b maybe.

def make_full_list(pos, c, n):
    out = [0.0]*n
    for i,val in zip(pos,c):
        if val > 0.0:
            out[int(i)] = float(val)
    return out
```

Mass transfer with support fixed and binary position map:
```
def optimize_weights_maxtransfer(pos, c, n, rng, deadline, max_iters=5000):
    m = pos.size
    c = c.copy()
    c = c / c.sum()
    # Build position to index map: since pos sorted, for target use searchsorted each lag? We'll do each iteration.
    best_c = c.copy(); best_score = sparse_score(...)
    no_improve = 0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       score, b = ...
       lam = int(np.argmax(b))
       target = lam - pos
       idx = np.searchsorted(pos, target)
       valid = idx < m and pos[idx] == target and target >=0
       grad = np.zeros(m); grad[valid] = 2.0 * c[idx[valid]]
       # Candidate out: top 5 indices with largest grad and c>0
       out_candidates = np.argsort(-grad)[:min(5,m)]
       # Candidate in: top 5 indices with smallest grad (including zeros), maybe c small
       in_candidates = np.argsort(grad)[:min(5,m)]
       improved = False
       for io in out_candidates:
           if grad[io] <= 0 or c[io] <= 0: continue
           for ii in in_candidates:
               if ii == io: continue
               # Try fractions descending
               for frac in (0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125,0.015625):
                   delta = frac * c[io]
                   c2 = c.copy(); c2[io] -= delta; c2[ii] += delta
                   s2, _ = sparse_score_with_sums(...) # just score no b? can compute b for maybe best
                   if s2 < best_score:
                       c = c2; best_score=s2; improved=True; no_improve=0; break
               if improved: break
           if improved: break
       if not improved:
           # Try smooth logsumexp gradient step maybe small
           # Or break
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 20: break
    return best_c,best_score
```
This does many O(m^2) score evals. Out/in 5*5*5=125 per iter worst; too many. We can limit fractions [0.1,0.01]. Maybe one out/in pair. Simpler: pick one out argmax, one in argmin; try fractions [0.1,0.05,0.02]. If no, break or try next pairs. Need balance.

Original c. After: c_i' = c_i-δ, c_j'=c_j+δ.
For any lag λ, change Δb_λ = -2δ r_i(λ) + 2δ r_j(λ) - 2δ c_i? Wait include self? Let's use q_λ = ∑_a c_a c_b with positions. Change from i,j:
Term involving i: old c_i*(c_i + ∑_{t≠i} c_t at partner) etc. The gradient for moving small δ: -δ ∂q/∂c_i + δ ∂q/∂c_j plus second order terms: -δ^2 for λ=2p_i -2δ? Actually if δ small, Δ ≈ δ(g_j - g_i), where g=∂q for current max? But other lags.

- We maintain c on simplex. Precompute sums matrix.

  - If no improvement, maybe try gradient on `grad - mean` or log-sum-exp changes too small; break.

But `project_simplex(c - lr*grad)` with lr=α/gradmax subtracts up to α (0.05) from c scaled by grad normalized to max 1. Good. It may reduce c at high grad and increase? Actually subtract positive grad can make c negative, projection will set to zero and distribute? Subtracting grad moves c vector in negative grad direction. Since all grad nonnegative, c - lr*grad reduces all components, with varying amounts; projection onto simplex adds a constant and zeros small components. This tends to reduce high-grad components more than low-grad, effectively moving mass from high to low? But it also reduces all; projection normalizes, so relative high-grad reduced more. Good.

```
scale = grad / (grad.max()+eps)
logw = -alpha * scale  # alpha 0.1-1
c_try = c * np.exp(logw)
c_try = c_try / c_try.sum()
```
This reduces high grad multiplicatively, keeps low. Since c sum changed, normalization redistributes mass to lower grad (implicitly). This is similar to mirror descent. It may be effective. Let's use both: multiplicative and linear project.

Mirror descent step:
- `g = (W @ c)` (without 2), nonnegative.
- Normalize direction: `g_max = g.max()+eps`; `ratios = c * np.exp(-eta * g/g_max)`; normalize. For eta e.g. 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3.
This changes c away from high responsibility. Since components with g=0 not reduced, their relative weight increases. Good.
- Evaluate exact score; accept if better.

This is O(m^2) for W+c and score. We can combine: we already have b and W? For W we need b weights; g uses same weights. Score can be computed from b (already have). So each evaluation one b, one W, candidate g. To test step, need new score (new b). Could do one candidate per iteration. Accept if better. Iterate.

Pseudo:
```
def optimize_weights_surrogate(pos,c,n,deadline,max_iters=2000):
    m=pos.size; c=c.copy(); c/=sum
    sums = pos[:,None]+pos[None,:].astype(np.int32)
    best_c=c.copy(); best_score=sparse_score_with_sums(...)
    no_imp=0
    eta_values = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]
    while time... and no_imp<20:
       score,b = get_b and score
       # if score improved when called? track
       beta = ...
       logits = beta*(b - b.max()); w = exp(logits); w/=sum
       W = w[sums]
       g = W @ c
       gm = g.max()+1e-300
       # choose a candidate eta based on current no_imp?
       for eta in eta_values:
           c_try = c * np.exp(-eta * g / gm)
           # If underflow all zero, fallback
           s_try = c_try.sum()
           if s_try <= 0: continue
           c_try = c_try / s_try
           score_try, _ = sparse_score_with_sums(pos, sums, c_try, n)
           if score_try < best_score:
               c = c_try; best_score=score_try; best_c=c.copy(); no_imp=0; break
       else:
           # try linear project maybe
           grad = 2*g
           for lr in [0.02,0.01,0.005]:
               c_try = project_simplex(c - lr * grad / (gm+1e-300))
               score_try,_ = ...
               if score_try < best_score: accept
           if not accept: no_imp+=1
    return best_c,best_score
```
Need `sparse_score_with_sums` not allocate b? It returns score,b. For candidate we only need score; but okay.

Could optimize by not computing b for candidate? We could update b O(m^2) anyway. Fine.

Memory: sums int32. For m~2000, 4M int32=16MB; W 32MB; okay.

Potential problem: weights W@ c for beta large and w concentrated at one lag, g is partner c at that lag. This is same as subgradient. Multiplicative update reduces peaks contributing to max; should help.

If support has many zero-grad components, their relative weights increase, possibly concentrating mass on few non-contributing peaks, which might create new high peaks. Line search exact prevents degradation.

Position optimization:
After weight opt, we can mutate support. But random already good. Still implement a simple hill-climb:
- Represent support positions and weights. We can add/move/remove mass.
- Operation types:
  1. Move a fraction of weight from an existing position i to a random new position q (could be empty or existing). If new, support increases by 1 (if weight small); if existing, combine and if source zero remove.
  2. Split a position: move fraction to another.
  3. Merge small weights.
- Candidate q selection:
   - Current b available; max lag lam. We want q such that q+pos not in high lags. Choose q from a set of low "load" positions. We can compute load for all N positions: `load[q] = max?` or sum of current b at q+pos? For each q, added contribution peak ~ c*sum_{t} c_t at lags q+p_t. We want low max over t. Computing exactly for all q: for each q, max_t b? But we can approximate by `min` of current b? If we add a small amount δ at q, change to b at lag λ includes δ*(sum_t c_t if q+p_t=λ) + δ^2. The max increase roughly δ * max_t c_t over partners? Since each q+pos distinct? Actually if q+pos values, added terms at each q+p_t. The maximum lag increase is δ* max_t c_t (or 2δ c_t?) plus δ^2. Since c_t <= max_c. So any q increase by at most ~δ*max_c at several lags. But existing b at those lags matters: new max = max(old_max, max_t (b[q+p_t]+δ*c_t), 2q+δ^2). To choose q, we can sample random q and compute max_add = max_t b[q+p_t]+δ*c_t. This is O(m) per candidate. For m=1200 and many candidates okay.
   - We can precompute current b array length 2N. For candidate q and source i (removing δ), old b changes by both removal and addition. Simpler evaluate exact score after mutation O(m^2). But candidate generation can be random.

def try_position_move(pos,c, q, frac_source, n, sums? no because support changes):

def choose_low_load_q(b,pos,rng,n,num_samples=2000):
    # sample ints
    qs = rng.integers(0,n,size=num_samples)
    idx = qs[:, None] + pos[None, :]
    vals = b[idx]  # b length 2n-1, idx max 2n-2
    maxvals = vals.max(axis=1)
    min_idx = np.argmin(maxvals)
    return int(qs[min_idx]), maxvals
```

import numpy as np, time

BASE_N = 5582

def get_problem_n():
   try:
      seq = height_sequence_1
   except NameError:
      return BASE_N
   if isinstance(seq, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
      L = len(seq)
      if 1 <= L <= 10000:
         return int(L)
   return BASE_N

def normalize_weights(c):
   c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
   c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
   s = c.sum()
   if s <= 0: return None
   return c/s

def score_and_b_with_sums(pos, sums, c, n):
   # c normalized? returns score,b
   m = pos.size
   if m ==0: return inf, np.zeros(2*n-1)
   s = c.sum()
   if s <= 1e-15: return inf,...
   prods = c[:,None] * c[None,:]
   b = np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=prods.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
   score = float(2.0*n*b.max()/(s*s))
   return score,b

def score_and_b(pos,c,n):
   pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int32)
   c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
   m=pos.size
   if m==0: ...
   sums = pos[:,None] + pos[None,:]
   return score_and_b_with_sums(pos,sums,c,n)

def project_simplex(v, target=1.0): ...

def random_multinomial_candidate(n,k,rng):
   draws = rng.integers(0,n,size=k)
   counts = np.bincount(draws, minlength=n)
   pos = np.nonzero(counts)[0].astype(np.int32)
   c = counts[pos].astype(np.float64)
   c /= float(k)
   return pos,c

Implement `optimize_weights_surrogate` robustly:

```
def optimize_weights_surrogate(pos, c, n, deadline, max_iter=100000):
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=np.int32).copy()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    s = c.sum()
    if s <=0: return pos,c,np.inf
    c /= s
    m = pos.size
    if m <=1:
       best_score = 2.0*np.inf? If one peak, conv peak 1, score 2n? Actually if single peak height1, b[2p]=1, score=2n/(n? wait S=1) = 2n. Not best. But not happen.
    sums = pos[:,None] + pos[None,:]
    sums = sums.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    best_c = c.copy()
    best_score, b = score_and_b_with_sums(pos,sums,c,n)
    if not finite: return
    no_imp = 0
    eta_list = (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0)
    # We can choose beta
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and iter < max_iter and no_imp < 30:
        iter +=1
        # current score,b maybe from last; but if we accept, update.
        # If no_imp and current b stale? maintain b current.
        bmax = float(b.max()) if b.size else 0
        if bmax <=0: break
        beta = min(1.0e7, max(100.0, 1000.0 / bmax))
        logits = beta * (b - bmax)
        # To avoid exp overflow (logits <=0)
        w = np.exp(logits)
        wsum = w.sum()
        if wsum <=0 or not finite: w = np.ones(2*n-1)/(2*n-1)
        else: w /= wsum
        wf = w.astype(np.float32, copy=False)
        # W = wf[sums] ; g = W @ c32
        W = wf[sums]
        c32 = c.astype(np.float32, copy=False)
        g = W.dot(c32)
        g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
        gmax = float(g.max())
        if gmax <= 1e-300:
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        accepted = False
        # Try eta descending? Start moderate. Choose based on no_imp maybe smaller.
        for eta in eta_list:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
            # multiplicative update
            fac = np.exp(-eta * (g / gmax))
            c_try = c * fac
            s_try = c_try.sum()
            if s_try <= 1e-300: continue
            c_try = c_try / s_try
            score_try, b_try = score_and_b_with_sums(pos, sums, c_try, n)
            if score_try < best_score:
                c = c_try; best_c = c.copy(); best_score = score_try; b = b_try; no_imp = 0; accepted = True; break
        if not accepted:
            # Try linear project on normalized grad
            g_norm = g / gmax
            for lr in (0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005):
               if time deadline: break
               c_try = project_simplex(c - lr * g_norm, 1.0)
               score_try,b_try = score_and_b_with_sums(...)
               if score_try < best_score: accept
        if not accepted:
            no_imp += 1
    return pos,best_c,best_score
```
Potential issue: `W = wf[sums]` creates a float32 m x m. `W.dot(c32)` returns float32. Good. But if m=2000, W 4M*4=16MB. Good.
`score_and_b_with_sums` allocates prods again. Could compute score_try using c_try; okay.

Beta selection: If bmax = 1e-4, beta=1000/bmax=1e7 capped 1e7, beta*bmax=100. logits range [-100,0], exp fine. If b differences 1e-5, beta diff=100. Good. If bmax smaller? bmax average 9e-5; fine. If bmax huge, beta lower, less concentration. Could use `beta = min(1e6, max(10.0, 200.0 / bmax))`? Need concentration. If bmax 1e-4, 200/bmax=2e6, beta*bmax=200. Good. Cap maybe 1e7. Use `200.0/bmax`. For bmax 1e-4, 2e6. Good.

Maybe `np.exp(logits)` underflows many to 0, okay. `wsum` maybe exp(0)=1 plus small, so w dominated by max lags. Good.

Multiplicative update `c * exp(-eta*g/gmax)`: if eta=2, g/gmax up to2, factor min 0.018; mass shifts strongly. Line search accepts only if exact score improves. If no eta improves, no_imp.

Will this converge? Likely.

Could there be an issue that `g` from W@ c uses weights over all lags but not normalized by c? Good.

Position optimization implement:

```
def optimize_positions(pos,c,n,deadline,best_score,max_m=2500):
   pos=np.asarray(pos,dtype=np.int32).copy(); c=... normalized
   best_pos=pos; best_c=c; best_s=score,b
   no_improve=0
   max_sample_elems = 2000000
   iter=0
   while time < deadline and no_improve < 200:
      m=pos.size
      if m ==0: break
      score,b = score_and_b(pos,c,n)
      if score < best_s: update
      # choose low q
      nsamples = min(3000, max(200, max_sample_elems // max(1,m)))
      qs = rng? Need rng pass. We'll pass rng.
      idx = qs[:,None] + pos[None,:]
      vals = b[idx]
      maxvals = vals.max(axis=1)
      minrank = np.argsort(maxvals)[:10]
      # choose sources: hot based on b max
      lam = int(np.argmax(b))
      target = lam - pos
      sidx = np.searchsorted(pos,target)
      valid = sidx < m and pos[sidx] == target
      hot = np.where(valid)[0]
      if len(hot)==0: source_list = [int(np.argmax(c))]
      else: source_list = hot[np.argsort(-c[hot])[:5]]
      # Also include max weight source
      source_list = unique list + [argmax(c)]
      accepted=False
      for q_choice in qs[minrank]:
        if time deadline break
        q = int(q_choice)
        # existing?
        idxq = np.searchsorted(pos,q)
        existing = idxq < m and pos[idxq] == q
        if not existing and m >= max_m:
            # choose an existing idx with low maxvals from sampled existing
            cand_idx = rng.integers(0,m,size=min(500,m))
            vals_ex = b[pos[cand_idx,None] + pos[None,:]]
            best_ex = cand_idx[int(np.argmin(vals_ex.max(axis=1)))]
            q = int(pos[best_ex]); existing=True
        for source in source_list:
           if source == idxq and existing: continue
           for frac in (0.1,0.03,0.01):
              # apply move
              new_pos,new_c = apply_move(pos,c,q,source,frac,n,eps=1e-15)
              if new_pos is None: continue
              score_try,b_try = score_and_b(new_pos,new_c,n)
              if score_try < best_s:
                  pos=new_pos; c=new_c; best_pos=pos.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_s=score_try; no_improve=0; accepted=True
                  break
           if accepted: break
        if accepted: break
      if not accepted:
         # maybe try random q not low? increase no_improve
         no_improve +=1
   return best_pos,best_c,best_s
```

Need `apply_move` top-level:
```
def apply_move(pos,c,q,source,frac,eps=1e-15):
    m=pos.size
    if source<0 or source>=m: return None,None
    delta = frac * c[source]
    if delta <=0: return None,None
    c2 = c.copy()
    c2[source] -= delta
    # if c2[source] < eps: c2[source]=0.0
    if c2[source] < eps: c2[source]=0.0
    idxq = np.searchsorted(pos, q)
    existing = idxq < m and pos[idxq] == q
    if existing:
        c2[idxq] += delta
        mask = c2 > eps
        if not np.any(mask): return None,None
        return pos[mask].astype(np.int32), c2[mask]
    else:
        # remove zeros from original
        mask = c2 > eps
        pold = pos[mask]; cold = c2[mask]
        idx = np.searchsorted(pold, q)
        new_pos = np.insert(pold, idx, q)
        new_c = np.insert(cold, idx, delta)
        # normalize? If zeros dropped, sum less; renormalize
        s = new_c.sum()
        if s <=0: return None,None
        if abs(s-1.0)>1e-12:
           new_c = new_c / s
        return new_pos, new_c
```

Let's revisit the prompt's SOTA approach:
Given f nonnegative normalized sum=√(2n). M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP maximizing ∑ b_j subject to b_j≥0 and ||f*b||∞≤M. Rescale b to normalization, blend f←(1-t)f+t b. Repeat.

This is a kind of subgradient method for minimizing M on the simplex. The LP direction: find g in the set of nonnegative vectors with circular? linear convolution with f bounded by M, maximizing sum. Since objective score = 2n M / S^2, with fixed normalization S=√(2n), minimizing M. The LP finds a point in the ellipsoid/polytope {g≥0, (f*g)_k≤M} with largest sum; normalized it is the Frank-Wolfe direction. If current f has max M, the LP's feasible set includes f? Wait f*f has max M, so f is feasible. LP maximizes sum over feasible set; the optimum g likely has sum > current S, after rescaling back it moves in direction that pushes M down? This is the away/steepest? They say repeating nonincreasing M.

Thus dense optimization feasible.

Algorithm dense projected gradient/mirror descent:
- Initialize a from a good parametric family (maybe U-shaped or uniform perturbed). Could start from random dense? But direct optimization may avoid local minima with good start.
- Normalize sum=1, clip nonnegative.
- Iterate:
   1. b = np.convolve(a,a,mode='full') (or use FFT? direct okay)
   2. score=2N*b.max().
   3. If improved update best.
   4. Choose beta: focus on top lags. `beta = min(1e6, max(10, 50 / (b.max()-b_second?)))`? We want weights over high lags. If beta too high, subgradient at single max; too low smooths all. Use `beta = 50 / (b.max() - np.partition(b, -50).min() + eps)`? Or simply `beta = 500000`? b scale ~1/N=1.8e-4. Difference maybe 1e-6. beta=1e6 gives differences 1-100. Could use `beta = 1000.0 / bmax` giving beta*bmax=1000; logits relative to max = beta*(b-bmax) ∈ [-1000,0]. This concentrates on top few. For N=5582, okay. Maybe adaptive: start low beta (e.g. 1000/bmax) and increase.
   5. w = softmax(beta*(b-bmax)) (exp, normalize).
   6. grad = 2 * np.correlate(w, a, mode='valid') (length N). Should be nonnegative? w,a nonnegative, yes.
   7. Update a in direction reducing high-responsibility. Use mirror descent: `a_try = a * exp(-eta * grad / grad.max())`; normalize. Or linear projected simplex: `a_try = project_simplex(a - lr*grad/gradmax)`. For dense N=5582, projection O(N log N) okay. Mirror descent keeps all positive (may underflow), can be effective. We can try a set of eta/lr, exact score.
   8. Maybe also use weight redistribution from high grad to low grad.

This is analogous to sparse weight opt but dense. It may find good nonnegative sequences.

What initialization? Uniform is score2. Need escape. We can initialize with a U-shaped density to reduce center. For example, `a_i = (i+1)^p + (N-i)^p` normalized, with p maybe -0.5 (edge-enhanced) or positive? Let's test continuous intuition: Edge-enhanced may increase edge peaks but reduce center. We can try a range of p and also monotone, and pick best after quick evaluation. Then run dense optimizer from best.

Parametric families:
- Uniform: score 2.
- U-shaped power: `w = (i+ε)^(p) + (N-1-i+ε)^(p)`, p maybe negative (singular) or positive (more edge if p>0? If p>0, edges have small i -> small values, center high (bell) not U. For U, need p<0 or use 1/(1+...)**. We can use `w = 1/(i+1+eps) + 1/(N-i+eps)`. Also more moderate: `w = 1/(1+ (i-c)^2/scale^2)`? That's center bell. U: `w = i^{alpha} + (N-1-i)^{alpha}` with alpha<0; or `w = exp(-alpha*dist)`? Center bell. We can try `w = 1/(1 + (i/(N-1)) ** q) + 1/(1 + ((N-1-i)/(N-1)) ** q)`? This is U for q positive (small denominator near edges? At edge (i=0) first term 1, at center both 1/2 ->1; no strong U). Use `w = (i/(N-1))^{-alpha} + ((N-1-i)/(N-1))^{-alpha}` = (x^{-α}+(1-x)^{-α}) with α>0 gives U (infinite edges if α>0; finite if cap). Discrete cap with +1.
- Two independent exponentials: `w = exp(-γ * min(i, N-1-i))`? That's center bell (exp small at edges). `w = exp(-γ * max(i, N-1-i))` edge? `w = exp(-γ * (1 - min(x,1-x)))` = exp(γ*min) is U (large at edges? min small at edges, exp(0)=1; center min=0.5 exp(0.5γ)>1, so center bell actually). We want high edges: use min distance to nearest edge d=min(i,N-1-i); edge d=0, center d=N/2. Edge high => exp(-γ d) (center bell) no; use exp(γ d) (center high). Hmm. U-shape high at edges and center low: value ∼ 1/(1+d)^α or exp(-γ d)? At edge d=0 value1, center small. Yes edge high. But discrete convolution of edge high may cause edge peaks. We can tune γ.
- Maybe optimal is not U but a mix: two broad humps near edges? We can initialize with random smooth functions and optimize.

Since dense optimizer can move, a good init may be enough. We can generate multiple inits:
1. Uniform.
2. Cosine modulated: `a = 1 + ε cos(π i/(N-1))`? This is U or bell depending sign. If ε positive, center? cos(0)=1 edges, cos(π)=-1 center => U (if ε<1 nonnegative with zero center). This is a smooth U-shaped density: a_i ∝ 1 + 0.9 cos(π i/(N-1)) (large edges, zero center). Or 1 - 0.9 cos(...) (bell). Try both.
3. Gaussian humps at edges: `exp(-((i-L)/σ)^2)+exp(-((N-1-L)/σ)^2)`.
4. Random smooth: random nonnegative vector with low-pass filtering to avoid huge peaks.
5. Sparse replacement random (from earlier) as fallback.

Then run dense optimization with projected gradient for a time, keep best.

Dense update approach:
```
def dense_optimize(a, n, deadline, max_iter, beta_shift=...):
    a = a.copy(); a = np.maximum(a,0); a/=sum
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_dense(a)
    no_imp=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       b=conv(a,a)
       score=2n*bmax/(sum^2) (sum=1)
       if score<best: update
       beta = choose
       logits=beta*(b-bmax); w=exp; w/=sum
       grad = 2*np.correlate(w, a, mode='valid')
       # grad may be length N
       gmax=grad.max()
       if not finite or 0: break
       # Normalize direction
       dir = grad / gmax
       accepted=False
       # Mirror descent steps
       for eta in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0):
           fac = np.exp(-eta*dir)
           a_try = a*fac
           s=a_try.sum()
           if s<=0: continue
           a_try/=s
           score_try = eval_dense(a_try)
           if score_try<best_score: accept
       if not accept:
           # Linear projected steps
           for lr in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2):
              a_try = project_simplex(a - lr*dir, 1.0)
              score_try=eval
              accept...
       if not accept:
           # maybe try pushing mass from high grad to low grad explicitly
           # or beta lower/higher
           no_imp+=1
           if no_imp>50: break
    return best,best_score
```
But mirror descent with dir nonnegative: `a*exp(-eta dir)` reduces all, more where dir high, then normalization increases relative low. For eta large, may underflow high components to zero. Good.

Direct max-lag transfer algorithm dense:
```
def maxlag_transfer(a, n, eta_list):
    b = conv(a,a); lam = argmax(b)
    idx = np.arange(n)
    partner = lam - idx
    valid = (partner>=0)&(partner<n)&(idx+partner? Actually for each i, if partner in range, term a[i]*a[partner] contributes; derivative =2*a[partner] if partner != i? If partner=i, derivative 2*a[i] still formula 2*a[partner]=2a[i], ok. But if partner==i, the product a_i^2 appears once derivative 2a_i, same.
    grad = np.zeros(n); grad[valid]=2*a[partner[valid]]
    i_out = argmax(grad), i_in = argmin(grad) (maybe grad=0)
    for frac: move a[i_in]+=delta, a[i_out]-=delta; if a_try nonnegative normalize? Moving preserves sum; if a_out goes below 0, use frac≤1.
    evaluate.
```
This is very O(N) plus conv. It directly addresses max lag. It may be powerful. We can loop: after each accepted, recompute b. If no improvement for max-lag, try transferring to top few low grad or random in. Also try multiple lags (top K) by weighting grad = sum_{λ topK} 2*a[λ-i]. This is like top-k subgradient but sparse in lag, can compute grad by looping over K lags and adding shifted a: O(K*N). For K up to 50, 5582*50=279k, fast. This may be better than correlate with softmax (which uses all lags weights but dense O(N^2) or FFT). Top-K lag subgradient:
```
top_lags = np.argpartition(b, -K)[-K:]
grad = zeros(N)
for lam in top_lags:
    partner = lam - np.arange(N)
    valid = ...
    grad[valid] += 2*a[partner[valid]]
```
This approximates subgradient of average top-K lags. Then move mass high to low or projected gradient. This is O(KN) per iteration, with K maybe 20-100. No O(N^2). Candidate evaluation still conv O(N^2). But we can choose K to balance.

Dense optimization routine revised:
```
def optimize_dense(a, n, time_limit):
    a normalized
    best_score = score; b=conv
    K_options = [5,10,20,50]
    no_imp=0
    for it while time:
       # choose K = K_options[no_imp mod len] maybe random
       top = argpartition(b, -K)[-K:]
       grad = zeros(N)
       for lam in top:
           partner = lam - np.arange(N)
           valid = ...
           grad[valid] += 2*a[partner[valid]]
       gmax=grad.max()
       dir=grad/gmax
       # Try updates: mass transfer from argmax dir to argmin dir with fractions; mirror; linear project.
       # Maybe first direct transfer fast (no normalization? sum preserved)
       for frac in [0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]:
          i=argmax(dir); j=argmin(dir)
          if dir[i] - dir[j] very small continue
          delta=frac*a[i]
          a_try=a.copy(); a_try[i]-=delta; a_try[j]+=delta
          score_try=dense_score(a_try)
          accept
       # Mirror/linear several eta
       for eta in ...:
          a_try=a*np.exp(-eta*dir); normalize; score
       for lr in ...:
          a_try=project_simplex(a - lr*dir)
       if no accept: no_imp++; if >100 break or random perturb
```

Let's implement dense optimizer with a loop over top-K subgradient and candidate updates, plus periodic random restarts/perturbations. We'll allocate significant time.

Potential bug: `np.argpartition(b, -K)` when K= len? Use if K>=len. K small.

Random perturbations:
```
def perturb_work(a, rng, sigma=0.05):
    noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, size=a.size)
    a_try = a * np.exp(noise)
    # also random low frequency? 
    s = a_try.sum()
    if s <=0: return a.copy()
    return a_try/s
```
If score_try < best + 0.2 accept as working for escape. We can do when no_imp > threshold.

But if working becomes too bad (score > 2), revert to best.

Global best: We can maintain `global_best_a` and score. In `optimize_dense`, we accept into working if score_try < working_score + tolerance, but update global if < global_best. Return best within this run (could be global best). To avoid losing, we can if working_score > global_best_score*1.2, reset working to global best.

Simpler: Each candidate tried must improve working (hill climbing) but we use multiple starts. Escaping via random perturb that worsens working but not global. We can track working_score.

Pseudo for optimize_dense:
```
def optimize_dense(init, n, L, deadline, global_best_score):
    a = normalize(init)
    working_score, b = score(a)
    if working_score < global_best_score: global update
    no_imp=0
    K_options=[3,7,15,30,60]
    k_idx=0
    anneal = 0.2
    while time<deadline:
       if no_imp > 30:
           # random perturbation
           a_try = perturb(a, rng)
           s_try,_=score(a_try)
           if s_try < working_score + max(0.05, anneal) and s_try < 2.5:
               a=a_try; working_score=s_try; b=...
           no_imp = max(0, no_imp-10)
           anneal = max(0.01, anneal*0.8)
       # compute topK grad
       K = K_options[k_idx % len]; k_idx++
       grad = topk_grad(b,a,K,n)
       # try candidate updates in order; for each if score < working_score + accept_tol (maybe 0 for early, small later)
       accept_tol = 0.0 if working_score < global_best_score + 0.1 else anneal*0.5
       ...
       if accepted:
           no_imp=0; anneal=max(0.01, annel*0.5?)
       else:
           no_imp+=1
    return best_a,best_score
```
But if we accept worsened working, we need b for it. Candidate score returns b; use.

- Scalar: out max, in min frac [0.1,0.03]

Let's define kernel `make_kernel(W, mode)`:
- Uniform: ones(W).
- Triangular: `1 - abs(np.arange(W) - (W-1)/2) / ((W-1)/2 + 1e-9)`, clip.
- Raised cosine: `0.5*(1+cos(2π*(np.arange(W)-0.5)/W))` maybe nonnegative, zero at edges.
- Gaussian: exp(-0.5*((x-(W-1)/2)/(W/6))^2).

Multi-hump generation details:
```
def add_bump(a, center, kernel):
    R = len(kernel)//2
    start = center - R
    # slice bounds
    s0=max(0,start); s1=min(N,start+len(kernel))
    k0=s0-start; k1=k1...
    a[s0:s1] += kernel[k0:k1]
```

Implementation details for `dense_b`:
```
def dense_b(a,n,L):
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=L)[:2*n-1]
    # clean tiny negative
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)
    return b
```
If a is normalized, b sum ~1; due FFT maybe slight. Score:
```
def dense_score_b(a,n,L):
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return np.inf, None
    b = dense_b(a,n,L)
    return float(2.0*n*b.max()/(s*s)), b
```
For speed in candidate, if we know a normalized sum1, can omit s. But robust.

`project_simplex`: standard. Need ensure target positive.
```
def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if v.size == 0: return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    r = np.arange(1, v.size+1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (u - (cssv - target) / r) > 0
    if not np.any(cond):
        return target / v.size + v? wait if no cond, rho = v.size? Let's use standard:
    rho = int(np.argmax(cond)) if any else len(v)-1
    theta = (cssv[rho] - target) / (rho+1)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
If no cond, all u <= (cumsum-target)/r. For positive target less than sum? Should always some cond if sum v > target? But if all v negative and target positive, cond? (u - (cssv-target)/r). sum v maybe less; simplex projection with target>sum v: need add? But our v = a - lr*dir, sum=1 - lr*sum(dir)? dir normalized? sum(dir) not 1, so sum(v) may be <1. `topk_grad`:
```
def topk_grad(b,a,K,n):
    if K >= b.size: idx=np.arange(b.size)
    else: idx=np.argpartition(b, -K)[-K:]
    grad=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
    idx=np.arange(n,dtype=np.int64)
    for lam in idx:
        p = lam - idx
        valid = (p>=0)&(p<n)
        grad[valid] += 2.0 * a[p[valid]]
    return grad
```
Potential issue: For each i, b_lam derivative = 2*a[lam-i] if valid; if partner index p equals i? still 2a_i correct. Good. This gradient is for sum of top lags equally. If top lags include invalid? b length 2n-1.

def dense_optimize(init, n, L, deadline, rng, global_best_score):
    a = normalize(init,n)
    score, b = dense_score_b(a,n,L)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
    current_score=score; current_b=b
    no_imp=0
    K_seq=[3,5,8,13,21,34]
    ki=0
    anneal=0.1
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it +=1
        # If current score too bad relative to best, reset
        if current_score > best_score + 0.5:
            a=best_a.copy(); current_score=best_score; current_b,? Need recompute b for best; cache best_b? We didn't. Could recompute.
        K=K_seq[ki % len(K_seq)]; ki+=1
        # Ensure b corresponds to a, maybe recompute if no b? We have current_b from score. After perturb, have.
        if current_b is None or current_score!=...: current_score,current_b=dense_score_b(a,n,L)
        grad=topk_grad(current_b,a,K,n)
        gmax=grad.max()
        if not finite or gmax==0: no_imp++; continue
        dir=grad/gmax
        # Candidate evaluation function top-level? Can't nest; define helper `try_candidate`? It would close? We can write inline loops.
        accepted=False
        # Candidate list generation with variables.
        # Scalar transfer:
        out_idx=int(np.argmax(dir))
        in_idx=int(np.argmin(dir))
        for frac in (0.1,0.05,0.02):
            delta=frac*a[out_idx]
            if delta <=0: continue
            a_try=a.copy(); a_try[out_idx]-=delta; a_try[in_idx]+=delta
            s_try,b_try=dense_score_b(a_try,n,L)
            if s_try < best_score:
                best_score=s_try; best_a=a_try.copy()
            if s_try < current_score + (anneal if no_imp>10 else 0.0):
                a=a_try; current_score=s_try; current_b=b_try; accepted=True; no_imp=0; break
        if not accepted:
           # mirror
           for eta in (0.05,0.15,0.4):
              fac=np.exp(-eta*dir)
              a_try=a*fac
              s=a_try.sum()
              if s<=1e-15: continue
              a_try/=s
              s_try,b_try=...
              update...
           if not accepted: linear lr (0.05,0.2)
        if not accepted:
            no_imp +=1
            anneal = max(0.02, anneal*0.9)
            if no_imp > 25:
                # random perturbation
                a_try=perturb_smooth(a,rng,0.05+0.02*min(no_imp,10))
                s_try,b_try=...
                if s_try < max(best_score, current_score) + anneal and s_try < 2.5:
                   a=a_try; current_score=s_try; current_b=b_try
                no_imp=0
                anneal=0.1
        if it % 200 ==0: print(...)
    # Maybe at end a bit local scalar from best? return best
    return best_a,best_score
```

Random perturbation `perturb_smooth`:
```
def perturb_smooth(a, rng, sigma):
    # low-pass random multiplicative noise
    noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, size=a.size)
    # smooth noise by simple moving average? time cost
    a_try = a * np.exp(noise)
    s=a_try.sum()
    if s<=0: return a.copy()
    return a_try/s
```
For smooth noise, use FFT: generate random white, keep low modes. Simpler: `noise = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(rng.normal(0,1,n,L)) * mask)[:n]`; mask low first 20 frequencies. But each perturb cost. We can just use moving average: `kernel = np.ones(50)/50; noise = np.convolve(noise,kernel,'same')`. O(N*50)=280k, okay occasional. Use top-level `smooth_perturb`.
```
def perturb_smooth(a,rng,sigma):
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=a.size)
    # smooth with boxcar
    w=50
    if w>1:
       k=np.ones(w)/w
       noise=np.convolve(noise,k,mode='same')
    a_try=a*np.exp(noise)
    s=a_try.sum()
    if s<=1e-15: return a.copy()
    return a_try/s
```
But sigma after smoothing reduced; okay.

```
def make_multi_hump(n,K,W,model,centers,rng):
   kernel=make_kernel(W,model)
   a=zeros(n)
   for c in centers: add_bump
   s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
   return a
```

`project_simplex` numeric:
```
def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    r = np.arange(1, v.size + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (u - (cssv - target) / r) > 0.0
    if not np.any(cond):
        rho = v.size - 1
    else:
        rho = int(np.argmax(cond))
    theta = (cssv[rho] - target) / (rho + 1.0)
    out = v - theta
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out
```
This projects onto sum <=? Standard for target. If sum(v)<target, theta negative, out all plus.

`make_kernel`:
```
def make_kernel(w, kind):
    if w <=1: return np.ones(1)
    x = np.arange(w, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 0:
       k = np.ones(w)
    elif kind == 1:
       c=(w-1)/2.0
       t=1.0 - np.abs(x-c)/(c+1e-12)
       k=np.maximum(t,0.0)
    elif kind == 2:
       k=0.5*(1.0+np.cos(2.0*np.pi*(x-0.5)/w))
       # zeros at edges? at x=0: cos(-π/w) not exactly0? (x-0.5)/w=-0.5/w, cos small angle ~1 => k~0.999, not zero. Actually raised cosine window often 0.5*(1+cos(2π x/w - π)) =0.5*(1-cos(2πx/w))? For nonnegative with zeros at edges: k=0.5*(1+cos(2π*x/w)) for x=0 ->1, x=w-1 ~? Use two-sided raised: k = 0.5*(1+cos(2π*(x/(w-1)-0.5)))? At endpoints x=0: cos(-π)= -1 =>0; x=w-1: cos(π)= -1 =>0; middle 1. Use if w>1:
           z = x/(w-1) - 0.5
           k = 0.5*(1+np.cos(2*np.pi*z))
       Good.
    else:
       c=(w-1)/2; sigma=max(1.0, w/6.0); k=np.exp(-0.5*((x-c)/sigma)**2)
    s=k.sum()
    if s>0: k/=s
    else: k=np.ones(w)/w
    return k.astype(np.float64)
```
Raised cosine is zero at endpoints, minimizes L2? Good.

`add_bump`:
```
def add_bump(a, center, kernel):
    n=a.size; w=kernel.size; R=w//2
    start=center-R
    s0=max(0,start); s1=min(n, start+w)
    if s0>=s1: return
    k0=s0-start; k1=k0+(s1-s0)
    a[s0:s1] += kernel[k0:k1]
```

```
def build_bumps(centers,kernel,n):
    a=np.zeros(n)
    for c in centers: add_bump(a,int(c),kernel)
    s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
    return a
```

def bump_center_local_search(centers,kernel,n,L,deadline,rng):
    centers=centers.astype(np.int64).copy()
    a=build_bumps(centers,kernel,n); score=dense_score...
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_centers=centers.copy()
    R=kernel.size//2; low=R+1; high=n-R-2
    offsets=[-50,-20,-7,-2,-1,1,2,7,20,50]
    it=0
    while time < deadline:
      it+=1
      improved=False
      # choose centers in random order or all
      order=np.arange(centers.size)
      rng.shuffle(order) # numpy rng.shuffle modifies in place, allowed
      for i in order:
        c=int(centers[i])
        for off in offsets:
           nc=c+off
           if nc<low or nc>high: continue
           # duplicate check
           dup=False
           for j in range(centers.size):
              if j!=i and centers[j]==nc: dup=True; break
           if dup: continue
           newc=centers.copy(); newc[i]=nc
           a_try=build_bumps(newc,kernel,n)
           s_try=score_only(a_try,n,L)
           if s_try < score - 1e-12:
              centers=newc; a=a_try; score=s_try; improved=True
              if s_try < best_score: best... 
              break
        if time >= deadline: break
      if not improved:
         # random shift one center
         i=int(rng.integers(0, centers.size))
         off=int(rng.integers(-200,201))
         nc=centers[i]+off; bounds; unique; eval; maybe allow if <score+0.05? For hill no.
         if not eval improved: break
    return best_a,best_score
```

We need pass `n` to `topk_grad`; `idx` array can be precomputed in dense_optimize to avoid each time. Top-level? In dense_optimize, create `idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)` and pass to a helper `topk_grad_with_idx`. Define helper `topk_grad(b,a,K,n,idx)`.

```
def topk_grad(b,a,K,n,idx):
   if K >= b.size: lags=np.arange(b.size)
   else: lags=np.argpartition(b,-K)[-K:]
   grad=np.zeros(n)
   for lam in lags:
      p=lam-idx
      valid=(p>=0)&(p<n)
      grad[valid]+=2.0*a[p[valid]]
   return grad
```

Parametric beta grid:
```
def add_beta_inits(top,n,L):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   xeps=np.maximum(x,1e-6); yeps=np.maximum(1-x,1e-6)
   vals = [-1.0,-0.7,-0.4,0.0,0.4,0.7,1.0,1.5]
   for alpha in vals:
      for beta in vals:
         v=xeps**alpha * yeps**beta
         add_init(v)
   # one-sided powers
   for p in vals: add xeps**p; add yeps**p
   # linear asym
   for q in np.linspace(-0.9,0.9,9): v=1+q*(x); add; v=1+q*(1-x); add
   # cosine
   for eps in [0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8]:
      v=1+eps*np.cos(np.pi*x); add if min>0
      v=1-eps*np.cos(np.pi*x); add
      v=1+eps*np.cos(2*np.pi*x); add
      v=1-eps*np.cos(2*np.pi*x); add
```

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative step-function height sequence with low
2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)**2).

Main ingredients:
1.  Many structured initial candidates:
    - beta/power/cosine/Gaussian profiles,
    - small multi-bump "Sidon-like" bump superpositions.
2.  FFT-based exact sparse score evaluations for the full length.
3.  A dense projected-gradient / mirror-descent optimizer for the
    nonnegative-simplex constraint, driven by top-lag subgradients.
4.  Local center search for the multi-bump constructions.

The returned list is the best sequence found within the time budget.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import operator

BASE_N = 5582


def normalize_array(a):
    """Nonnegative normalize to sum one."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def next_fft_len(n):
    """Power-of-two FFT length large enough for linear convolution."""
    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1
    return L


def dense_b_fft(a, n, L):
    """Linear self-convolution via real FFT."""
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, n=L)[:2 * n - 1]
    # Remove tiny negative roundoff.
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)
    return b


def score_and_b(a, n, L):
    """Score and convolution vector for a sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf, None
    b = dense_b_fft(a, n, L)
    score = float(2.0 * n * b.max() / (s * s))
    return score, b


def score_only(a, n, L):
    """Score only, for candidate screening."""
    score, _ = score_and_b(a, n, L)
    return score


def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    """Project v onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=target}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    r = np.arange(1, v.size + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (u - (cssv - target) / r) > 0.0
    if not np.any(cond):
        rho = v.size - 1
    else:
        rho = int(np.argmax(cond))
    theta = (cssv[rho] - target) / (rho + 1.0)
    out = v - theta
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def keep_top(top_list, score, a, maxn=20):
    """Maintain a small list of best candidates without lambda."""
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if len(top_list) < maxn:
        top_list.append((float(score), a))
        return
    worst_i = 0
    worst_v = top_list[0][0]
    for i in range(1, len(top_list)):
        if top_list[i][0] > worst_v:
            worst_v = top_list[i][0]
            worst_i = i
    if score < worst_v:
        top_list[worst_i] = (float(score), a)


def keep_params(top_list, score, centers, kernel, maxn=20):
    """Maintain best multi-bump parameters."""
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    centers = np.asarray(centers, dtype=np.int64).copy()
    kernel = np.asarray(kernel, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if len(top_list) < maxn:
        top_list.append((float(score), centers, kernel))
        return
    worst_i = 0
    worst_v = top_list[0][0]
    for i in range(1, len(top_list)):
        if top_list[i][0] > worst_v:
            worst_v = top_list[i][0]
            worst_i = i
    if score < worst_v:
        top_list[worst_i] = (float(score), centers, kernel)


def add_initial(top_list, a, n, L, maxn=20):
    """Normalize, score, add to top list if useful."""
    if a is None:
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size != n:
        return
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return
    a = a / s
    sc = score_only(a, n, L)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        keep_top(top_list, sc, a, maxn)


def profile_inits(top_list, n, L):
    """Generate several simple smooth profiles."""
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    xeps = np.maximum(x, 1e-6)
    yeps = np.maximum(1.0 - x, 1e-6)

    # Uniform fallback.
    add_initial(top_list, np.ones(n), n, L)

    # Asymmetric power / beta-like profiles.
    pows = [-1.0, -0.7, -0.4, -0.15, 0.15, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5]
    for p in pows:
        add_initial(top_list, xeps ** p, n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, yeps ** p, n, L)
    for a1 in pows:
        for a2 in pows:
            add_initial(top_list, xeps ** a1 * yeps ** a2, n, L)

    # Linear asymmetric.
    qs = np.linspace(-0.9, 0.9, 19)
    for q in qs:
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 + q * x, n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 + q * (1.0 - x), n, L)

    # Cosine profiles.
    eps_vals = [0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 0.95]
    for e in eps_vals:
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 + e * np.cos(np.pi * x), n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 - e * np.cos(np.pi * x), n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 + e * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x), n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 - e * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x), n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 + e * np.cos(3.0 * np.pi * x), n, L)
        add_initial(top_list, 1.0 - e * np.cos(3.0 * np.pi * x), n, L)

    # Gaussian bumps, single and paired.
    centers = [0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.8, 0.9]
    widths = [0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.35, 0.5]
    for c in centers:
        for w in widths:
            v = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - c) / w) ** 2)
            add_initial(top_list, v, n, L)
    pairs = [(0.15, 0.85), (0.2, 0.8), (0.25, 0.75), (0.3, 0.7), (0.35, 0.65)]
    for c1, c2 in pairs:
        for w in widths:
            v = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - c1) / w) ** 2) + np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - c2) / w) ** 2)
            add_initial(top_list, v, n, L)


def make_kernel(w, kind):
    """Normalized positive kernel of width w."""
    w = max(1, int(w))
    if w == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    t = np.arange(w, dtype=np.float64)
    if kind == 0:
        k = np.ones(w, dtype=np.float64)
    elif kind == 1:
        c = 0.5 * (w - 1)
        k = 1.0 - np.abs(t - c) / (c + 1e-12)
        k = np.maximum(k, 0.0)
    elif kind == 2:
        z = t / (w - 1.0) - 0.5
        k = 0.5 * (1.0 + np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * z))
        k = np.maximum(k, 0.0)
    else:
        c = 0.5 * (w - 1)
        sigma = max(1.0, w / 6.0)
        k = np.exp(-0.5 * ((t - c) / sigma) ** 2)
    s = k.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        k = np.ones(w, dtype=np.float64) / w
    else:
        k = k / s
    return k.astype(np.float64)


def add_bump_to_array(a, center, kernel):
    """Add a centered kernel slice into array a."""
    n = a.size
    w = kernel.size
    half = w // 2
    start = int(center) - half
    s0 = max(0, start)
    s1 = min(n, start + w)
    if s0 >= s1:
        return
    k0 = s0 - start
    k1 = k0 + (s1 - s0)
    a[s0:s1] += kernel[k0:k1]


def build_bumps(centers, kernel, n):
    """Build normalized bump superposition."""
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for c in centers:
        add_bump_to_array(a, c, kernel)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def sample_bump_centers(k, low, high, rng):
    """Sample k distinct centers."""
    if k <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)
    if k == 1:
        return np.array([int(rng.integers(low, high + 1))], dtype=np.int64)
    centers = []
    used = set()
    attempts = 0
    while len(centers) < k and attempts < 200 * k:
        attempts += 1
        c = int(rng.integers(low, high + 1))
        if c not in used:
            used.add(c)
            centers.append(c)
    # Fallback: fill evenly if random failed.
    if len(centers) < k:
        fallback = np.linspace(low, high, k).astype(np.int64)
        seen = set(centers)
        for c in fallback:
            if len(centers) >= k:
                break
            c = int(c)
            if c not in seen:
                seen.add(c)
                centers.append(c)
    return np.asarray(centers, dtype=np.int64)


def even_bump_centers(k, low, high):
    """Evenly spaced distinct centers."""
    centers = np.linspace(low, high, k).astype(np.int64)
    # Repair duplicates by small shifts.
    for i in range(1, k):
        if centers[i] == centers[i - 1]:
            centers[i] += 1
        if centers[i] > high:
            centers[i] = high - i + 1
    return centers


def multi_bump_inits(top_list, params_top, n, L, rng, max_attempts=700):
    """Generate random/even multi-bump candidates."""
    ks = [5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 13, 16, 20, 25]
    factors = [1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0, 9.0]
    kinds = [0, 1, 2, 3]
    attempts = 0

    for k in ks:
        max_w = max(9, n // 2)
        for factor in factors:
            w = int(round(factor * n / float(k * k)))
            if w % 2 == 0:
                w += 1
            w = max(5, min(w, max_w))
            kernel = make_kernel(w, 0)
            half = w // 2
            low = half + 1
            high = n - half - 2
            if high <= low:
                continue

            # Try 3 random and 1 even center set for each kind.
            for kind in kinds:
                kr = make_kernel(w, kind)
                for trial in range(4):
                    if attempts >= max_attempts:
                        return
                    attempts += 1
                    if trial == 0:
                        centers = even_bump_centers(k, low, high)
                    else:
                        centers = sample_bump_centers(k, low, high, rng)
                    a = build_bumps(centers, kr, n)
                    if a is None:
                        continue
                    sc = score_only(a, n, L)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        keep_top(top_list, sc, a, 30)
                        keep_params(params_top, sc, centers, kr, 20)


def bump_center_local_search(centers, kernel, n, L, deadline, rng):
    """Hill-climb bump centers for fixed kernel."""
    centers = np.asarray(centers, dtype=np.int64).copy()
    k = centers.size
    if k == 0:
        return np.zeros(n) / n, np.inf

    a = build_bumps(centers, kernel, n)
    if a is None:
        return np.ones(n) / n, np.inf
    score = score_only(a, n, L)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    best_centers = centers.copy()

    half = kernel.size // 2
    low = half + 1
    high = n - half - 2
    offsets = [-80, -30, -10, -4, -2, -1, 1, 2, 4, 10, 30, 80]

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        improved = False
        order = np.arange(k)
        rng.shuffle(order)

        for i in order:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            orig = int(centers[i])
            for off in offsets:
                nc = orig + off
                if nc < low or nc > high:
                    continue
                duplicate = False
                for j in range(k):
                    if j != i and int(centers[j]) == nc:
                        duplicate = True
                        break
                if duplicate:
                    continue
                new_centers = centers.copy()
                new_centers[i] = nc
                new_a = build_bumps(new_centers, kernel, n)
                if new_a is None:
                    continue
                new_score = score_only(new_a, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < score - 1e-12:
                    centers = new_centers
                    a = new_a
                    score = new_score
                    improved = True
                    if new_score < best_score:
                        best_score = new_score
                        best_a = new_a.copy()
                        best_centers = centers.copy()
                    break

        if not improved:
            # Occasional bigger random kick, but only accept if it helps.
            i = int(rng.integers(0, k))
            off = int(rng.integers(-250, 251))
            nc = int(centers[i]) + off
            if nc < low or nc > high:
                break
            duplicate = False
            for j in range(k):
                if j != i and int(centers[j]) == nc:
                    duplicate = True
                    break
            if duplicate:
                break
            new_centers = centers.copy()
            new_centers[i] = nc
            new_a = build_bumps(new_centers, kernel, n)
            if new_a is None:
                break
            new_score = score_only(new_a, n, L)
            if np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < score - 1e-12:
                centers = new_centers
                a = new_a
                score = new_score
            else:
                break

    return best_a, best_score


def top_lag_gradient(b, a, k_top, n, idx):
    """Subgradient for average of top k_top convolution lags."""
    if k_top >= b.size:
        lags = np.arange(b.size)
    else:
        lags = np.argpartition(b, -k_top)[-k_top:]
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for lam in lags:
        partner = int(lam) - idx
        valid = (partner >= 0) & (partner < n)
        grad[valid] += 2.0 * a[partner[valid]]
    return grad


def smooth_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    """Smooth multiplicative random perturbation."""
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=a.size)
    w = 41
    if w > 1:
        k = np.ones(w, dtype=np.float64) / float(w)
        noise = np.convolve(noise, k, mode="same")
    a_try = a * np.exp(noise)
    s = a_try.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return a.copy()
    return a_try / s


def dense_optimize(init_a, anchor_a, anchor_score, n, L, deadline, rng):
    """
    Nonconvex weighted simplex optimizer.

    Uses top-lag subgradients, mirror-descent steps, projected
    linear steps, and occasional smooth random perturbations.
    """
    a = normalize_array(init_a)
    if a is None:
        a = anchor_a.copy()
        score = anchor_score
        b = None
    else:
        score, b = score_and_b(a, n, L)
        if not np.isfinite(score) or b is None:
            a = anchor_a.copy()
            score = anchor_score
            score, b = score_and_b(a, n, L)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    current_score = score
    current_b = b

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    k_options = [4, 7, 12, 20, 34, 55]
    ki = 0
    no_imp = 0
    anneal = 0.12
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        # If the working point escapes too far, reset to the best known here.
        if current_score > best_score + 0.6:
            a = best_a.copy()
            current_score = best_score
            current_score, current_b = score_and_b(a, n, L)
            no_imp = 0
            anneal = 0.12

        if current_b is None:
            current_score, current_b = score_and_b(a, n, L)

        k_top = k_options[ki % len(k_options)]
        ki += 1
        grad = top_lag_gradient(current_b, a, k_top, n, idx)
        gmax = float(grad.max())
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-300:
            no_imp += 1
            continue
        direction = grad / gmax

        accepted = False
        accept_tol = 0.0
        if no_imp > 5:
            accept_tol = max(0.02, 0.5 * anneal)
        if no_imp > 15:
            accept_tol = anneal

        def _accept_if_good(a_try, s_try, b_try):
            # This is a nested helper in current draft; replace by inlined code below.
            pass

        # Inlined candidate tests (no nested functions in final code).
        # 1) Direct mass transfer from high-gradient to low-gradient.
        out_idx = int(np.argmax(direction))
        in_idx = int(np.argmin(direction))
        if out_idx != in_idx and a[out_idx] > 0.0:
            for frac in (0.1, 0.05, 0.02):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                delta = float(frac) * float(a[out_idx])
                if delta <= 0.0:
                    continue
                a_try = a.copy()
                a_try[out_idx] -= delta
                a_try[in_idx] += delta
                s_try, b_try = score_and_b(a_try, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(s_try) and np.isfinite(b_try is None):
                    # b_try None only if score not finite; b_try is array if finite.
                    pass
                if np.isfinite(s_try):
                    if s_try < best_score:
                        best_score = s_try
                        best_a = a_try.copy()
                    if s_try < current_score + accept_tol and s_try < best_score + 0.35:
                        a = a_try
                        current_score = s_try
                        current_b = b_try
                        accepted = True
                        no_imp = 0
                        anneal = max(0.02, anneal * 0.55)
                        break
        if not accepted:
            # 2) Mirror-descent multiplicative updates.
            for eta in (0.08, 0.2, 0.5, 1.2):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                fac = np.exp(-eta * direction)
                a_try = a * fac
                s_try_val = a_try.sum()
                if s_try_val <= 1e-300:
                    continue
                a_try = a_try / s_try_val
                s_try, b_try = score_and_b(a_try, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(s_try):
                    if s_try < best_score:
                        best_score = s_try
                        best_a = a_try.copy()
                    if s_try < current_score + accept_tol and s_try < best_score + 0.35:
                        a = a_try
                        current_score = s_try
                        current_b = b_try
                        accepted = True
                        no_imp = 0
                        anneal = max(0.02, anneal * 0.55)
                        break

        if not accepted:
            # 3) Projected linear updates.
            for lr in (0.04, 0.12, 0.3):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                a_try = project_simplex(a - lr * direction, 1.0)
                s_try, b_try = score_and_b(a_try, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(s_try):
                    if s_try < best_score:
                        best_score = s_try
                        best_a = a_try.copy()
                    if s_try < current_score + accept_tol and s_try < best_score + 0.35:
                        a = a_try
                        current_score = s_try
                        current_b = b_try
                        accepted = True
                        no_imp = 0
                        anneal = max(0.02, anneal * 0.55)
                        break

        if not accepted:
            # 4) Transfer to a few alternative low-gradient positions.
            if a.size > 0:
                in_candidates = np.argpartition(direction, min(4, n) - 1)[:4]
                out_cand = int(np.argmax(direction))
                for in_idx in in_candidates:
                    if in_idx == out_cand:
                        continue
                    if a[out_cand] <= 0.0:
                        break
                    for frac in (0.08, 0.03):
                        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                            break
                        delta = float(frac) * float(a[out_cand])
                        if delta <= 0.0:
                            continue
                        a_try = a.copy()
                        a_try[out_cand] -= delta
                        a_try[int(in_idx)] += delta
                        s_try, b_try = score_and_b(a_try, n, L)
                        if np.isfinite(s_try):
                            if s_try < best_score:
                                best_score = s_try
                                best_a = a_try.copy()
                            if s_try < current_score + accept_tol and s_try < best_score + 0.35:
                                a = a_try
                                current_score = s_try
                                current_b = b_try
                                accepted = True
                                no_imp = 0
                                anneal = max(0.02, anneal * 0.55)
                                break
                    if accepted:
                        break

        if not accepted:
            no_imp += 1
            anneal = max(0.02, anneal * 0.9)
            if no_imp > 18:
                # Random smooth escape move.
                a_try = smooth_perturb(a, rng, 0.08 + min(0.25, 0.02 * no_imp))
                s_try, b_try = score_and_b(a_try, n, L)
                if np.isfinite(s_try) and s_try < max(current_score, best_score) + anneal + 0.05:
                    a = a_try
                    current_score = s_try
                    current_b = b_try
                no_imp = 0
                anneal = min(0.25, anneal * 2.0)

        if it % 250 == 0:
            print("dense_it", it, "score", float(best_score), flush=True)

    # Final quick verification and return.
    final_score = score_only(best_a, n, L)
    if np.isfinite(final_score) and final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
    return best_a, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Returns a list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    # Construction length from problem statement. If a compatible global
    # construction exists, use its length; otherwise use 5582.
    n = BASE_N
    try:
        base = height_sequence_1
        if isinstance(base, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
            lb = int(len(base))
            if 1000 <= lb <= 9000:
                n = lb
    except Exception:
        n = BASE_N

    n = max(1, int(n))
    L = next_fft_len(n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    deadline = start + max(60.0, float(budget_s)) - 20.0

    top_sequences = []
    bump_params = []

    # 0) Use any compatible previous construction as one candidate.
    try:
        base = height_sequence_1
        if isinstance(base, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)) and len(base) == n:
            add_initial(top_sequences, np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64), n, L, 30)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # 1) Smooth profile candidates.
    profile_inits(top_sequences, n, L)

    # 2) Multi-bump random/Sidon-like candidates.
    multi_bump_inits(top_sequences, bump_params, n, L, rng, max_attempts=800)

    # Sort sequences by score.
    if top_sequences:
        top_sequences.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    if bump_params:
        bump_params.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

    # 3) Local center search on best bump constructions.
    if bump_params and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Give up to ~25% of remaining time to bump center search, capped.
        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
        bump_time = min(300.0, max(30.0, 0.25 * remaining))
        per = max(10.0, bump_time / max(1, min(len(bump_params), 5)))
        for i in range(min(len(bump_params), 5)):
            sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + per
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            _, centers, kernel = bump_params[i]
            a_c, s_c = bump_center_local_search(centers, kernel, n, L, sub_deadline, rng)
            if s_c is not None and np.isfinite(s_c):
                keep_top(top_sequences, s_c, a_c, 30)

    # Re-sort after bump search.
    if top_sequences:
        top_sequences.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

    # 4) Dense optimization on the best initial sequences.
    if not top_sequences:
        # Guaranteed fallback init.
        a_f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        top_sequences.append((score_only(a_f, n, L), a_f))

    best_a = top_sequences[0][1].copy()
    best_score = float(top_sequences[0][0])
    print("best_after_initials", float(best_score), flush=True)

    runs = top_sequences[:8]
    if runs and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
        # Allocate longer slices to better initializations.
        scores = [max(1e-12, abs(x[0])) for x in runs]
        total_inv = sum(1.0 / s for s in scores)
        if total_inv > 0:
            for a_init, sc in runs:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                frac = (1.0 / max(1e-12, sc)) / total_inv
                # Clamp slice: at least 30s if possible, at most 700s.
                slice_s = remaining * frac
                slice_s = max(30.0, min(700.0, slice_s))
                sub_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + slice_s)
                a_best_run, s_best_run = dense_optimize(
                    a_init, best_a, best_score, n, L, sub_deadline, rng
                )
                if np.isfinite(s_best_run) and s_best_run < best_score:
                    best_score = s_best_run
                    best_a = a_best_run.copy()
                    print("new_best", float(best_score), flush=True)

    # 5) One final short dense polish from the global best.
    if time.monotonic() + 45.0 < deadline:
        a_pol, s_pol = dense_optimize(
            best_a, best_a, best_score, n, L,
            min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 45.0),
            rng
        )
        if np.isfinite(s_pol) and s_pol < best_score:
            best_score = s_pol
            best_a = a_pol.copy()

    # Safety: ensure valid finite nonnegative list.
    best_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_a[~np.isfinite(best_a)] = 0.0
    best_a = np.maximum(best_a, 0.0)
    if best_a.size != n:
        best_a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    s = best_a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        best_a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    else:
        best_a = best_a / s
        # Keep values far below the evaluator's 1000 cap.
        cap = 100.0
        if best_a.max() > cap:
            best_a = best_a * (cap / best_a.max())

    out = best_a.tolist()

    # Final exact evaluation if available. If it is invalid, fall back to uniform.
    try:
        ev = evaluate_sequence(out)
        if not np.isfinite(ev):
            raise ValueError("invalid eval")
    except Exception:
        fallback = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        out = fallback.tolist()
        try:
            evaluate_sequence(out)
        except Exception:
            pass

    return out
```
